Let's detail the WMMA usage per warp for 32x32 C tile: Each warp does: - C_frag: 2x2 = 4 accumulators of shape m16n16k16. For each K step of 16: - Load A_frag_a (2 of m16k16, for rows 0..31) and A_frag_b (2 of m16k16, for cols 0..15). Actually, we need to load A and B fragments from shared memory. A_smem has 128x32. A warp needs a 32x16 slice. We'll load using `wmma::load_matrix_sync`. For warp_id: warp_row = warp_id % 4 (0..3) -> offset in M: warp_row * 32. warp_col = warp_id / 4 (0..1) -> offset in N: warp_col * 32. C_frag covers rows [warp_row*32, warp_row*32+31] and cols [warp_col*32, warp_col*32+31]. Then process two steps of 16 each (K=0..1 inside the loop). This amortizes the global memory loads. Wait, K=129. If we tile by 32, we have 5 iterations: 0..31, 32..63, 64..95, 96..127, 128..128 (1 element). The last one is tricky. We can just handle the remainder. However, WMMA requires K to be multiple of 16 for the fragments. For the last partial tile, we can pad with zeros in shared memory. Since we load from global to shared, we can fill the padding with 0.0. So the algorithm: For each block: - Determine blockBatch, blockRow, blockCol. - If out of bounds, return. - Initialize 4 C_frag accumulators to 0.0f (FP32 accumulator). - Loop over K in steps of `BK=32`: - Load A tile: size `BM x BK` from global memory to shared memory. A is (BATCH, M, K) row-major in PyTorch. So A[b, m, k] index = b*M*K + m*K + k. Wait, PyTorch tensor is (BATCH, M, K). In memory, it's row-major for last dim: contiguous in K. So `A[b,m,k]` offset is `b*M*K + m*K + k`. However, GEMM is C[b,m,n] = sum_k A[b,m,k] * B[b,k,n]. So A is accessed by (m,k) with stride K. B is accessed by (k,n) with stride N. This is actually standard row-major batched GEMM! A shape: (B, M, K), stride: (M*K, K, 1) B shape: (B, K, N), stride: (K*N, N, 1) C shape: (B, M, N), stride: (M*N, N, 1) This is perfect. We don't need to worry about column-major transposes. - Load B tile: `BK x BN`. - __syncthreads. - For k_step = 0; k_step < BK; k_step += 16: Each warp loads A_frag[2] from smem (m=32, k=16). Each warp loads B_frag[2] from smem (k=16, n=32). mma_sync pairs. - __syncthreads. - After K loop, store C_frag to smem or directly to global? Since C is 128x64 per block, and each warp writes 32x32, we can write directly to global with bounds checking. But WMMA store writes to shared or global? `wmma::store_matrix_sync` can write to global directly, but it's better to write to a shared memory scratchpad and then have threads write to global to handle beta*C0 and alpha scaling. Let's write the kernel using WMMA. Actually, writing a robust WMMA kernel for arbitrary M,N,K with remainder handling is complex but doable. Let's outline the kernel: ```cpp #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <mma.h> using namespace nvcuda; constexpr int WMMA_M = 16; constexpr int WMMA_N = 16; constexpr int WMMA_K = 16; constexpr int BLOCK_M = 128; // tile size in M constexpr int BLOCK_N = 64; // tile size in N constexpr int BLOCK_K = 32; // tile size in K constexpr int WARP_M = 32; // each warp tile constexpr int WARP_N = 32; constexpr int NUM_WARPS_M = BLOCK_M / WARP_M; // 4 constexpr int NUM_WARPS_N = BLOCK_N / WARP_N; // 2 constexpr int NUM_WARPS = NUM_WARPS_M * NUM_WARPS_N; // 8 constexpr int THREADS_PER_BLOCK = NUM_WARPS * 32; // 256 __global__ void batched_gemm_wmma( const half* __restrict__ A, const half* __restrict__ B, const half* __restrict__ C0, float* __restrict__ C, int M, int N, int K, int batch_count, float alpha, float beta) { // A: (batch, M, K) // B: (batch, K, N) // C0: (batch, M, N) // C: (batch, M, N) int batch = blockIdx.z; if (batch >= batch_count) return; int block_row = blockIdx.x * BLOCK_M; int block_col = blockIdx.y * BLOCK_N; // Warp id within block int warp_id = threadIdx.x / 32; int lane_id = threadIdx.x % 32; int warp_row = warp_id % NUM_WARPS_M; int warp_col = warp_id / NUM_WARPS_M; int warp_row_offset = block_row + warp_row * WARP_M; int warp_col_offset = block_col + warp_col * WARP_N; // Early exit if entire warp tile is out of bounds? Or handle per fragment. // We'll handle per fragment during store. wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_a, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, half, wmma::row_major> a_frag[2]; // 2 along M wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_b, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, half, wmma::row_major> b_frag[2]; // 2 along N // Wait, standard GEMM: A row-major (M,K), B row-major (K,N). // WMMA uses A: row_major, B: col_major by default for m16n16k16? Actually for m16n16k16, you can specify layout. // For row-major A and row-major B, the math is still correct if we treat them appropriately. // In wmma, if A is row_major and B is col_major, it's standard. // But B is row-major in memory. If we specify B as row_major in wmma, does it work? // wmma::fragment<matrix_b, M, N, K, half, wmma::row_major> works on Hopper? I think so. // Actually, the original wmma only supported matrix_b with col_major for fp16. Wait, on older archs, B had to be col_major for certain configurations. For m16n16k16, B can be row_major? Let's check. // Actually, according to docs: For matrix_a, layout can be row_major or col_major. For matrix_b, layout can be row_major or col_major. // But for m16n16k16, both are supported. // Let's use row_major for both since our data is row-major. wmma::fragment<wmma::accumulator, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, float> acc_frag[2][2]; // 2x2 WMMA tiles per warp for (int i=0; i<2; i++) for (int j=0; j<2; j++) wmma::fill_fragment(acc_frag[i][j], 0.0f); __shared__ half As[BLOCK_M][BLOCK_K]; // row-major shared memory __shared__ half Bs[BLOCK_K][BLOCK_N]; // Pointers to current batch const half* A_batch = A + batch * M * K; const half* B_batch = B + batch * K * N; const half* C0_batch = C0 + batch * M * N; float* C_batch = C + batch * M * N; for (int bk = 0; bk < K; bk += BLOCK_K) { // Load A tile (BLOCK_M x BLOCK_K) to As int num_threads = THREADS_PER_BLOCK; int a_elems = BLOCK_M * BLOCK_K; for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < a_elems; idx += num_threads) { int sm_r = idx / BLOCK_K; int sm_c = idx % BLOCK_K; int gl_r = block_row + sm_r; int gl_c = bk + sm_c; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (gl_r < M && gl_c < K) { val = A_batch[gl_r * K + gl_c]; } As[sm_r][sm_c] = val; } // Load B tile (BLOCK_K x BLOCK_N) to Bs int b_elems = BLOCK_K * BLOCK_N; for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < b_elems; idx += num_threads) { int sm_r = idx / BLOCK_N; int sm_c = idx % BLOCK_N; int gl_r = bk + sm_r; int gl_c = block_col + sm_c; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (gl_r < K && gl_col < N) { val = B_batch[gl_r * N + gl_col]; } Bs[sm_r][sm_c] = val; } __syncthreads(); // Compute on this K tile for (int k_step = 0; k_step < BLOCK_K; k_step += WMMA_K) { // Load a_frag for warp rows // a_frag[0]: rows warp_row*32 + 0..15, cols k_step..k_step+15 // a_frag[1]: rows warp_row*32 + 16..31, cols k_step..k_step+15 int a_row_0 = warp_row * WARP_M; int a_row_1 = warp_row * WARP_M + WMMA_M; wmma::load_matrix_sync(a_frag[0], &As[a_row_0][k_step], BLOCK_K); wmma::load_matrix_sync(a_frag[1], &As[a_row_1][k_step], BLOCK_K); int b_col_0 = warp_col * WARP_N; int b_col_1 = warp_col * WARP_N + WMMA_N; wmma::load_matrix_sync(b_frag[0], &Bs[k_step][b_col_0], BLOCK_N); wmma::load_matrix_sync(b_frag[1], &Bs[k_step][b_col_1], BLOCK_N); // MMA for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) { for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) { wmma::mma_sync(acc_frag[i][j], a_frag[i], b_frag[j], acc_frag[i][j]); } } } __syncthreads(); } // Store and apply alpha/beta // Each fragment acc_frag[i][j] corresponds to: // rows: warp_row_offset + i*WMMA_M + local_m // cols: warp_col_offset + j*WMMA_N + local_n // We need to write to C_batch. // We can store fragments to shared memory or directly write. // Since we need to do alpha*acc + beta*C0, and C0 is in global memory, // it's easiest to store fragment to a local array and then write to global. // But wmma::store_matrix_sync needs a memory pointer. We can use a small shared memory scratchpad per warp or thread-local array. // Actually, acc_frag.x[] is accessible per thread. // We can iterate over the elements of the fragment and write directly. // However, the mapping of fragment elements to threads is not trivial. Using store_matrix_sync to smem and then having threads read/write is standard. __shared__ float Csm[WARP_M * WARP_N]; // per warp? Too big for all warps simultaneously. // Alternative: each warp uses its own shared memory partition? We can allocate [NUM_WARPS][WARP_M][WARP_N]. __shared__ float Csm[NUM_WARPS][WARP_M][WARP_N]; for (int i=0; i<2; i++) { for (int j=0; j<2; j++) { int row_base = warp_row * WARP_M + i * WMMA_M; int col_base = warp_col * WARP_N + j * WMMA_N; wmma::store_matrix_sync(&Csm[warp_id][i*WMMA_M][j*WMMA_N], acc_frag[i][j], WARP_N, wmma::mem_row_major); } } __syncthreads(); // Wait for all warps to store. But actually each warp writes to its own area, no conflict. // Now each thread writes elements from Csm to global C. // We can divide the warp's tile among its threads. // WARP_M=32, WARP_N=32 => 1024 elements. 32 threads => 32 elements per thread. int warp_elems = WARP_M * WARP_N; for (int idx = lane_id; idx < warp_elems; idx += 32) { int r = idx / WARP_N; int c = idx % WARP_N; int gl_r = warp_row_offset + r; int gl_c = warp_col_offset + c; if (gl_r < M && gl_c < N) { float val = Csm[warp_id][r][c]; // Load C0 if needed float c0 = __half2float(C0_batch[gl_r * N + gl_c]); val = alpha * val + beta * c0; C_batch[gl_r * N + gl_c] = val; } } } ``` This is a basic outline. Let's refine it. Issues: 1. `As` and `Bs` shared memory dimensions: `As[BLOCK_M][BLOCK_K]` and `Bs[BLOCK_K][BLOCK_N]`. For 128x32 and 32x64, sizes are 4KB and 4KB in FP16. Total 8KB. Plus `Csm` for 8 warps * 32*32 floats = 8 * 1024 * 4 = 32KB. Total 40KB. This is well within 228KB. 2. The `wmma::load_matrix_sync` for B: `&Bs[k_step][b_col_0]`. Bs is `half[BK][BN]`. The leading dimension for load is `BLOCK_N`. This is correct if `Bs` is row-major and we pass column stride `BLOCK_N`. 3. `wmma::store_matrix_sync`: we need a row-major 2D array. `Csm[warp_id]` is a 2D array of size 32x32 floats. Leading dimension `WARP_N`. `wmma::mem_row_major`. Good. 4. Fragment accumulation in `float` is supported by WMMA. 5. Padding: When loading from global, out-of-bounds loads set to 0. Let's write the CUDA kernel with the following params: ``` __global__ void batched_gemm_wmma( const half* __restrict__ A, const half* __restrict__ B, const half* __restrict__ C0, float* __restrict__ C, int M, int N, int K, int batch_count, float alpha, float beta) ``` I'll use block sizes: ``` const int BM = 128; const int BN = 64; const int BK = 32; ``` Warps: 8 (256 threads). WARP_TILE_M = 32 WARP_TILE_N = 32 Thus warp layout 4x2. Let's now write the code carefully to avoid compilation errors. ```cpp #include <torch/types.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <mma.h> using namespace nvcuda; #define BM 128 #define BN 64 #define BK 32 #define WMMA_M 16 #define WMMA_N 16 #define WMMA_K 16 __global__ void batched_gemm_wmma_kernel( const half* __restrict__ A, const half* __restrict__ B, const half* __restrict__ C0, float* __restrict__ C, int M, int N, int K, int batch_count, float alpha, float beta) { int batch = blockIdx.z; if (batch >= batch_count) return; int block_row = blockIdx.x * BM; int block_col = blockIdx.y * BN; int warp_id = threadIdx.x / 32; int lane_id = threadIdx.x % 32; const int warps_m = BM / 32; // 4 const int warps_n = BN / 32; // 2 int warp_row = warp_id % warps_m; int warp_col = warp_id / warps_m; int warp_row_offset = block_row + warp_row * 32; int warp_col_offset = block_col + warp_col * 32; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_a, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, half, wmma::row_major> a_frag[2]; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_b, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, half, wmma::row_major> b_frag[2]; wmma::fragment<wmma::accumulator, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, float> acc_frag[2][2]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) wmma::fill_fragment(acc_frag[i][j], 0.0f); __shared__ half As[BM][BK]; __shared__ half Bs[BK][BN]; const half* A_batch = A + batch * M * K; const half* B_batch = B + batch * K * N; const half* C0_batch = C0 + batch * M * N; float* C_batch = C + batch * M * N; for (int bk = 0; bk < K; bk += BK) { // Load A tile #pragma unroll for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < BM * BK; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / BK; int c = idx % BK; int gl_r = block_row + r; int gl_c = bk + c; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (gl_r < M && gl_c < K) { val = A_batch[gl_r * K + gl_c]; } As[r][c] = val; } // Load B tile #pragma unroll for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < BK * BN; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / BN; int c = idx % BN; int gl_r = bk + r; int gl_c = block_col + c; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (gl_r < K && gl_c < N) { val = B_batch[gl_r * N + gl_c]; } Bs[r][c] = val; } __syncthreads(); // Multiply for (int k_step = 0; k_step < BK; k_step += WMMA_K) { int a_row_base = warp_row * 32; wmma::load_matrix_sync(a_frag[0], &As[a_row_base + 0][k_step], BK); wmma::load_matrix_sync(a_frag[1], &As[a_row_base + WMMA_M][k_step], BK); int b_col_base = warp_col * 32; wmma::load_matrix_sync(b_frag[0], &Bs[k_step][b_col_base + 0], BN); wmma::load_matrix_sync(b_frag[1], &Bs[k_step][b_col_base + WMMA_N], BN); #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) { for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) { wmma::mma_sync(acc_frag[i][j], a_frag[i], b_frag[j], acc_frag[i][j]); } } } __syncthreads(); } // Store to shared scratch per warp __shared__ float Csm[8][32][32]; // 8 warps, 32x32 tiles #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) { for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) { int row_base = warp_row * 32 + i * WMMA_M; int col_base = warp_col * 32 + j * WMMA_N; wmma::store_matrix_sync(&Csm[warp_id][i*WMMA_M][j*WMMA_N], acc_frag[i][j], 32, wmma::mem_row_major); } } // Write to global with alpha/beta #pragma unroll for (int idx = lane_id; idx < 32 * 32; idx += 32) { int r = idx / 32; int c = idx % 32; int gl_r = warp_row_offset + r; int gl_c = warp_col_offset + c; if (gl_r < M && gl_c < N) { float acc = Csm[warp_id][r][c]; float c0 = __half2float(C0_batch[gl_r * N + gl_c]); C_batch[gl_r * N + gl_c] = alpha * acc + beta * c0; } } } ``` Wait, `Csm` size: 8*32*32*4 = 32KB. `As` = 128*32*2 = 8KB. `Bs` = 32*64*2 = 4KB. Total 44KB. Fine. But there is a shared memory issue: `wmma::store_matrix_sync` requires the memory to be accessible to the warp. Since `Csm` is shared and each warp writes to its own slice, it's fine. However, `Csm[warp_id][i*WMMA_M][j*WMMA_N]` indexing: `Csm` is declared as `[8][32][32]`. The address of `Csm[warp_id][i*WMMA_M][j*WMMA_N]` is `&Csm[warp_id][0][0] + (i*WMMA_M)*32 + j*WMMA_N`. The leading dimension for `store_matrix_sync` is 32 (the width of the second dimension). This is correct because the row stride is 32 floats. Potential compile issue: `wmma::fragment<..., float>` for accumulator with `wmma::store_matrix_sync` to float memory. This is supported. Another issue: `wmma::load_matrix_sync` with row-major B. On Hopper, does WMMA support row_major for B? According to CUDA docs: "For matrix_b, the supported layouts are row_major and col_major for all architectures." Yes, row_major is supported. Let's verify with a simple check. If compilation fails, we can switch B to col_major by transposing the indexing. But let's assume row_major works. Also, we need to include proper headers. `torch/types.h` gives `at::Half`, but we can use `half` from `cuda_fp16.h`. When passing tensors from PyTorch, `half*` can be cast from `at::Half*` because `at::Half` is ABI-compatible with `__half`. To be safe, we can transpose the indexing of B in shared memory. If we store B in shared memory as transposed (i.e., `Bs[BN][BK]` instead of `BK][BN]`), then we can declare B as `col_major` and load with leading dimension `BK`. Let's examine: We want B(k, n) with k varying fastest in memory (row-major). For col_major, the memory layout is transposed: n varies fastest. The `batched_gemm_wmma_cuda` wrapper should check that inputs are contiguous and on CUDA. Code: ```python import torch import torch.nn as nn from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline CUDA_SOURCE = r''' #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <mma.h> using namespace nvcuda; constexpr int BM = 128; constexpr int BN = 64; constexpr int BK = 32; constexpr int WMMA_M = 16; constexpr int WMMA_N = 16; constexpr int WMMA_K = 16; __global__ void __launch_bounds__(256, 2) batched_gemm_wmma_kernel( const half* __restrict__ A, const half* __restrict__ B, const half* __restrict__ C0, float* __restrict__ C, int M, int N, int K, int batch_count, float alpha, float beta) { int batch = blockIdx.z; if (batch >= batch_count) return; int block_row = blockIdx.x * BM; int block_col = blockIdx.y * BN; int warp_id = threadIdx.x / 32; int lane_id = threadIdx.x % 32; constexpr int warps_m = BM / 32; // 4 constexpr int warps_n = BN / 32; // 2 int warp_row = warp_id % warps_m; int warp_col = warp_id / warps_m; int warp_row_offset = block_row + warp_row * 32; int warp_col_offset = block_col + warp_col * 32; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_a, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, half, wmma::row_major> a_frag[2]; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_b, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, half, wmma::row_major> b_frag[2]; wmma::fragment<wmma::accumulator, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, float> acc_frag[2][2]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) wmma::fill_fragment(acc_frag[i][j], 0.0f); __shared__ half As[BM][BK]; __shared__ half Bs[BK][BN]; const half* A_batch = A + (int64_t)batch * M * K; const half* B_batch = B + (int64_t)batch * K * N; const half* C0_batch = C0 + (int64_t)batch * M * N; float* C_batch = C + (int64_t)batch * M * N; for (int bk = 0; bk < K; bk += BK) { // Load A tile to shared memory #pragma unroll for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < BM * BK; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / BK; int c = idx % BK; int gl_r = block_row + r; int gl_c = bk + c; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (gl_r < M && gl_c < K) { val = A_batch[(int64_t)gl_r * K + gl_c]; } As[r][c] = val; } // Load B tile to shared memory #pragma unroll for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < BK * BN; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / BN; int c = idx % BN; int gl_r = bk + r; int gl_c = block_col + c; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (gl_r < K && gl_c < N) { val = B_batch[(int64_t)gl_r * N + gl_c]; } Bs[r][c] = val; } __syncthreads(); // Compute on this K tile for (int k_step = 0; k_step < BK; k_step += WMMA_K) { int a_row_base = warp_row * 32; wmma::load_matrix_sync(a_frag[0], &As[a_row_base + 0][k_step], BK); wmma::load_matrix_sync(a_frag[1], &As[a_row_base + WMMA_M][k_step], BK); int b_col_base = warp_col * 32; wmma::load_matrix_sync(b_frag[0], &Bs[k_step][b_col_base + 0], BN); wmma::load_matrix_sync(b_frag[1], &Bs[k_step][b_col_base + WMMA_N], BN); #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) { for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) { wmma::mma_sync(acc_frag[i][j], a_frag[i], b_frag[j], acc_frag[i][j]); } } } __syncthreads(); } // Shared scratchpad for storing accumulator before writing output __shared__ float Csm[8][32][32]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) { for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) { int row_base = warp_row * 32 + i * WMMA_M; int col_base = warp_col * 32 + j * WMMA_N; wmma::store_matrix_sync(&Csm[warp_id][row_base][col_base], acc_frag[i][j], 32, wmma::mem_row_major); } } // Write to global memory with alpha/beta #pragma unroll for (int idx = lane_id; idx < 32 * 32; idx += 32) { int r = idx / 32; int c = idx % 32; int gl_r = warp_row_offset + r; int gl_c = warp_col_offset + c; if (gl_r < M && gl_c < N) { float acc = Csm[warp_id][r][c]; float c0 = __half2float(C0_batch[(int64_t)gl_r * N + gl_c]); C_batch[(int64_t)gl_r * N + gl_c] = alpha * acc + beta * c0; } } } torch::Tensor batched_gemm_forward( torch::Tensor A, torch::Tensor B, torch::Tensor C0, float alpha, float beta) { int batch = A.size(0); int M = A.size(1); int K = A.size(2); int N = B.size(2); TORCH_CHECK(A.is_cuda(), "A must be CUDA"); TORCH_CHECK(B.is_cuda(), "B must be CUDA"); TORCH_CHECK(C0.is_cuda(), "C0 must be CUDA"); TORCH_CHECK(A.dtype() == torch::kHalf, "A must be half"); TORCH_CHECK(B.dtype() == torch::kHalf, "B must be half"); TORCH_CHECK(C0.dtype() == torch::kHalf, "C0 must be half"); auto C = torch::empty({batch, M, N}, A.options().dtype(torch::kFloat32)); dim3 threads(256); dim3 blocks((M + BM - 1) / BM, (N + BN - 1) / BN, batch); batched_gemm_wmma_kernel<<<blocks, threads>>>( reinterpret_cast<const half*>(A.data_ptr<at::Half>()), reinterpret_cast<const half*>(B.data_ptr<at::Half>()), reinterpret_cast<const half*>(C0.data_ptr<at::Half>()), C.data_ptr<float>(), M, N, K, batch, alpha, beta); return C; } ''' batched_gemm = load_inline( name="batched_gemm_wmma", cuda_sources=CUDA_SOURCE, functions=["batched_gemm_forward"], extra_cuda_cflags=["-O3", "--use_fast_math"], verbose=False, ).batched_gemm_forward class ModelNew(nn.Module): def __init__(self, BATCH, K, N, ALPHA, BETA): super().__init__() self.B = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(BATCH, K, N, dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda')) self.C0 = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(BATCH, 520, N, dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda')) self.alpha = ALPHA self.beta = BETA def forward(self, A): # A is float32 from get_inputs A_half = A.half() return batched_gemm(A_half, self.B, self.C0, self.alpha, self.beta) ``` Wait, `C0` shape uses hardcoded M=520. In `forward`, if A has M=520, it matches. If not, it fails. But `get_inputs` always returns M=520. To be slightly safer, I could create C0 on the fly: ```python def forward(self, A): batch, M, K = A.shape if M != self.C0.size(1): C0 = torch.ones(batch, M, self.B.size(2), dtype=torch.float16, device=A.device) else: C0 = self.C0 A_half = A.half() return batched_gemm(A_half, self.B, C0, self.alpha, self.beta) ``` This is safer and still fast. Here is a major bug! `Csm[warp_id][row_base][col_base]` where `row_base` is offset within the *block*, not within the warp tile. I incorrectly used `row_base = warp_row * 32 + i * WMMA_M` which is 0..31 for the warp. But `Csm[warp_id]` is only 32x32. `row_base` should be `i * WMMA_M` (0 or 16). Similarly `col_base = j * WMMA_N` (0 or 16). Wait, no: the warp tile is 32x32. The 4 WMMA fragments cover it: - i=0,j=0: rows 0..15, cols 0..15 -> store at `Csm[warp_id][0][0]` - i=0,j=1: rows 0..15, cols 16..31 -> store at `Csm[warp_id][0][16]` - i=1,j=0: rows 16..31, cols 0..15 -> store at `Csm[warp_id][16][0]` - i=1,j=1: rows 16..31, cols 16..31 -> store at `Csm[warp_id][16][16]` So `row_base` should be `i*WMMA_M` and `col_base` `j*WMMA_N`. There is no `warp_row * 32` inside `Csm` because `Csm` is per warp! The code had: ```cpp int row_base = warp_row * 32 + i * WMMA_M; ``` That's wrong. It should be `i * WMMA_M`. The write loop then uses `warp_row_offset` (which includes block and warp offsets) for global addresses. That part is correct. Fix store: ```cpp for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) { for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) { wmma::store_matrix_sync(&Csm[warp_id][i*WMMA_M][j*WMMA_N], acc_frag[i][j], 32, wmma::mem_row_major); } } ``` Yes. Let's fix that. Also, `wmma::load_matrix_sync` for A: `&As[a_row_base + 0][k_step]`. `a_row_base` is `warp_row * 32`. So for warp_row=3, it's 96. `As` has 128 rows. `a_row_base + WMMA_M = 112`. Both < 128. Good. For B: `&Bs[k_step][b_col_base + 0]`. `b_col_base` is `warp_col * 32`. Max `warp_col=1`, so `b_col_base=32`. `b_col_base + WMMA_N = 48`. But `Bs` has `BN=64` columns. 48 < 64. Good. One more detail: The write loop uses `Csm[warp_id][r][c]` with `r = idx / 32`, `c = idx % 32`. Since `idx < 1024`, `r` is 0..31. Good. Let's write the final code with the fix. I should also make sure the PyTorch extension compiles. `torch.utils.cpp_extension.load_inline` might need `extra_cuda_cflags=["-arch=sm_90", "-O3"]`. Since H100 is sm_90, adding the arch flag ensures the WMMA instructions target Hopper correctly. Without it, PyTorch might use sm_80 or whatever the default is. Let's add `-gencode=arch=compute_90,code=sm_90` or just `-arch=sm_90`. Actually, for H100, you want `sm_90`. Default might be okay if the driver JIT compiles, but explicit is better. However, the GPU might not support compiling for sm_90 if the toolchain is old. But H100 implies sm_90 capable system. I'll add it. ```python extra_cuda_cflags=["-O3", "-gencode=arch=compute_90,code=sm_90"] ``` Let's do a final review of the code before outputting.
assistant```python import torch import torch.nn as nn from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline CUDA_SOURCE = r''' #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <mma.h> using namespace nvcuda; // 2 int warp_row = warp_id % warps_m; int warp_col = warp_id / warps_m; int warp_row_offset = block_row + warp_row * 32; int warp_col_offset = block_col + warp_col * 32; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_a, WMMA_M, WMMA_N, WMMA_K, half, wmma::row_major> a_frag[2]; const half* C0_batch = C0 + (int64_t)batch * M * N; float* C_batch = C + (int64_t)batch * M * N; for (int bk = 0; bk < K; bk += BK) { // Load A tile to shared memory #pragma unroll for (int idx = threadIdx.x; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (gl_r < K && gl_c < N) { val = B_batch[(int64_t)gl_r * N + gl_c]; } Bs[r][c] = val; } __syncthreads(); // Compute on this K tile for (int k_step = 0; k_step < BK; k_step += WMMA_K) { int a_row_base = warp_row * 32; wmma::load_matrix_sync(b_frag[1], &Bs[k_step][b_col_base + WMMA_N], BN); #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 2; ++i) { for (int j = 0; j < 2; ++j) { wmma::mma_sync(acc_frag[i][j], a_frag[i], b_frag[j], acc_frag[i][j]); } } // Write to global memory with alpha/beta for (int idx = lane_id; idx < 32 * 32; idx += 32) { int r = idx / 32; int c = idx % 32; int gl_r = warp_row_offset + r; int gl_c = warp_col_offset + c; if (gl_r < M && gl_c < N) { float acc = Csm[warp_id][r][c]; float c0 = __half2float(C0_batch[(int64_t)gl_r * N + gl_c]); C_batch[(int64_t)gl_r * N + gl_c] = alpha * acc + beta * c0; } } } torch::Tensor batched_gemm_forward( torch::Tensor A, torch::Tensor B, torch::Tensor C0, float alpha, float beta) { int batch = A.size(0); TORCH_CHECK(B.dtype() == torch::kHalf, "B must be half"); TORCH_CHECK(C0.dtype() == torch::kHalf, "C0 must be half"); auto C = torch::empty({batch, M, N}, A.options().dtype(torch::kFloat32)); dim3 threads(256); dim3 blocks((M + BM - 1) / BM, (N + BN - 1) / BN, batch); batched_gemm_wmma_kernel<<<blocks, threads>>>( reinterpret_cast<const half*>(A.data_ptr<at::Half>()), reinterpret_cast<const half*>(B.data_ptr<at::Half>()), reinterpret_cast<const half*>(C0.data_ptr<at::Half>()), C.data_ptr<float>(), M, N, K, batch, alpha, beta); return C; } ''' batched_gemm = load_inline( name="batched_gemm_wmma", cuda_sources=CUDA_SOURCE, functions=["batched_gemm_forward"], extra_cuda_cflags=["-O3", "-gencode=arch=compute_90,code=sm_90"], verbose=False, ).batched_gemm_forward class ModelNew(nn.Module): def __init__(self, BATCH, K, N, ALPHA, BETA): super().__init__() self.N = N self.alpha = ALPHA self.beta = BETA self.B = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(BATCH, K, N, dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda')) # C0 is kept as a buffer for the most common M; recreated on the fly if M differs self.register_buffer('C0', torch.ones(BATCH, 520, N, dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda')) def forward(self, A): batch, M, K = A.shape if M != self.C0.size(1) or batch != self.C0.size(0): C0 = torch.ones(batch, M, self.N, dtype=torch.float16, device=A.device) else: C0 = self.C0 A_half = A.half() return batched_gemm(A_half, self.B, C0, self.alpha, self.beta) ```

reasoning
The output is incorrect because of a mismatch in memory layout assumptions. The issue is that the reference implementation uses a specific row-major or column-major layout. Let me reconsider. Looking at the original CUTLASS code, it uses `cutlass::layout::ColumnMajor` for all three matrices. The reference code shows: - A: (batch, m, k) with stride `lda * k` = `m * k` - B: (batch, k, n) with stride `k` - C: (batch, m, n) with stride `ldc * n` = `m * n` But wait, the PyTorch contract says: - A is (BATCH, M, K) which is row-major in PyTorch terms - B is stored as (BATCH, K, N) parameter - Output should match the reference In PyTorch, the default layout is row-major (contiguous in the last dimension). So A[b][m][k] has elements at A[b,m,k] = A_base + b*M*K + m*K + k. But CUTLASS ColumnMajor means: - A[m,k] in column-major: element at A[m,k] = A_base + m + k*lda - lda = m (from example) - So with M=520, K=129: A[520,129] col-major means A[m,k] at offset m + k*520 In PyTorch row-major: A[m,k] at offset m*K + k = m*129 + k So there's a layout difference. The original CUTLASS code assumes column-major, but PyTorch tensors are row-major. For the contract, the reference implementation probably handles this. The reference says `get_inputs()` returns `torch.randn(BATCH, M, K)` which is row-major. I need to figure out what the reference does. Since the problem mentions "batched strided gemm" and the original code had specific strides, the reference probably: 1. Interprets A as (batch, M, K) row-major (which is equivalent to column-major with transposed dimensions... wait no) Actually, looking at the CUTLASS example more carefully: ``` int const lda = m; int const ldb = k * batch_count; // This is weird, they interleave batches in B ``` Wait, in the example B has `ldb = k * batch_count` and `batch_stride_B = k`. That means B is arranged as: - B[k_idx + n_idx * ldb + b_idx * k] - ldb = k * batch = 129 * 17 = 2193 - So B[b, k, n] is at offset k + n * (k * batch) + b * k That's very strange. Let me reconsider... Actually, looking more carefully at the reference code for B: ```cpp for (int b_idx = 0; b_idx < batch_count; b_idx++) { for (int col_idx = 0; col_idx < n; col_idx++) { for (int row_idx = 0; row_idx < k; row_idx++) { host_B[row_idx + col_idx * ldb + b_idx * k] = ... ``` ldb = k * batch_count = 2193 So host_B is NOT (batch, K, N), it's arranged such that: B[b, k, n] at offset k + n * (k * batch) + b * k This is a strange layout. But in PyTorch, `self.B = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(BATCH, K, N))` would be standard row-major: B[b, k, n] at offset b*K*N + k*N + n. So the reference must be doing something to convert. The I/O contract says `get_inputs()` returns `[torch.randn(BATCH, M, K)]` and `get_init_inputs` is `[BATCH, K, N, ALPHA, BETA]`. The model stores B as a (BATCH, K, N) parameter and A is (BATCH, M, K). The reference probably uses standard PyTorch `torch.baddbmm` or `torch.matmul` for the batched gemm. If so: - C[b] = alpha * A[b] @ B[b] + beta * C0[b] - Where A[b] is (M,K), B[b] is (K,N), C0[b] is (M,N) In standard PyTorch, these are all row-major contiguous. But wait, the problem description says we need to produce equivalent output to a hidden reference. The reference is likely using `torch.bmm` or `torch.matmul`. Looking at the original CUTLASS reference more carefully again: - A is column-major with lda=m - B is column-major with ldb=k - C is column-major with ldc=m In column-major, a (M,K) matrix has elements at (m,k) -> base + m + k*lda. This is equivalent to row-major of the TRANSPOSED matrix. Hmm, but PyTorch uses row-major. So if A is (M,K) row-major in PyTorch, element at (m,k) -> base + m*K + k. If the reference uses standard PyTorch matmul, it computes C[b,m,n] = sum_k A[b,m,k] * B[b,k,n], which is standard row-major matrix multiplication. Let me just implement a standard row-major batched GEMM using WMMA. But WMMA on Hopper uses column-major for shared memory. Wait, actually WMMA now supports row-major too. For the Tensor Core on Hopper, the newer HMMA instructions and WGMMA support more flexible layouts. But using `wmma::row_major` for both A and B should work. Let me rewrite with proper row-major shared memory and standard batch semantics: - A is (batch, M, K) row-major - B is (batch, K, N) row-major - C0 is (batch, M, N) row-major - Output C is (batch, M, N) but in FP32 For a row-major batched GEMM: - C[b,m,n] = alpha * sum_k(A[b,m,k] * B[b,k,n]) + beta * C0[b,m,n] - A[b,m,k] at offset b*M*K + m*K + k - B[b,k,n] at offset b*K*N + k*N + n - C[b,m,n] at offset b*M*N + m*N + n Let me write a simpler tiled GEMM kernel without initially using WMMA, just to get correctness, then optimize. I need to make sure the thread layout covers the full tile without leaving gaps.

Actually, for a standard PyTorch `nn.Parameter(torch.randn(BATCH, K, N))`, the memory is row-major [B, K, N]. If a column-major routine reads it as KxN, the element at logical position (k, n) is at memory offset k + n*K. But in the row-major tensor, offset k + n*K corresponds to element [k / N, k % N, ...] or something. It's scrambled. To make the column-major routine read the correct logical values, we need the tensor to be stored as [N, K] row-major. That is, a tensor of shape [N, K] row-major has element [n, k] at offset n*K + k. When a column-major routine reads element (k, n), it goes to offset k + n*K. This is the same if K is the leading dimension... wait. For a column-major K×N matrix: offset(k, n) = k + n*lda, where lda = K. So offset = k + n*K. For a row-major N×K tensor: offset(n, k) = n*K + k. Yes! offset(n, k) in row-major N×K = offset(k, n) in column-major K×N. So if we want a column-major routine to read logical element (k, n) from a PyTorch tensor at the correct offset, the PyTorch tensor should be N×K row-major, and its values should be B[n, k] = desired_B[k, n]. In other words, if we store B^T (shape N×K) in a PyTorch tensor, and pass its pointer to a column-major K×N GEMM, then the GEMM reads the correct B values. Similarly for A: if A is M×K column-major, offset(m, k) = m + k*M. A PyTorch tensor of shape [K, M] row-major has offset(k, m) = k*M + m. This matches! Actually, there's a known trick: for row-major GEMM in cuBLAS, you can compute C.T = B.T @ A.T using column-major. Or equivalently: C_row = A_row @ B_row can be computed as: C_col^T = B_col^T @ A_col^T where ^T means transpose. No. Wait: if A_row^T = A_col (A stored as row-major in PyTorch vs column-major in cuBLAS), then: (A_row @ B_row)^T = B_row^T @ A_row^T = B_col @ A_col So C_row.T = B_col @ A_col. Thus, if we store C as column-major and compute C_col = B_col @ A_col, then C contains (A_row @ B_row).T. To get C_row = A_row @ B_row, we would need to transpose the result. For row-major A[b, M, K] and B[b, K, N], to get C[b, M, N] row-major: In cuBLAS column-major, this is equivalent to computing C.T = B.T @ A.T. where all matrices are viewed as column-major. Wait, no: a row-major [M, K] matrix is the transpose of a column-major [K, M] matrix. Actually, here's the key identity: A_row_major[M,K] = A_col_major^T[K,M] So if we pass A_row_major pointer as a column-major [K,M] matrix, then the logical values are A_col_major[k,m] = A_row_major[m,k]. For C = A @ B in row-major: C_row[m,n] = sum_k A_row[m,k] * B_row[k,n] = sum_k A_col^T[m,k] * B_col^T[k,n] = sum_k A_col[k,m] * B_col[n,k] If we view A as KxM col-major and B as NxK col-major: - A_col[k,m] with opA=CUBLAS_OP_T gives A_col^T = MxK, element (m,k) = A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k]. - B_col[n,k] with opB=CUBLAS_OP_T gives B_col^T = KxN, element (k,n) = B_col[n,k] = B_row[k,n]. Then cuBLAS computes: C_col = opA(A_col) @ opB(B_col) = A_col^T @ B_col^T = (MxK) @ (KxN) = MxN. C_col[m,n] = sum_k A_col^T[m,k] * B_col^T[k,n] = sum_k A_row[m,k] * B_row[k,n] = C_row[m,n]. So the logical result is C_row! But cuBLAS stores it as column-major MxN. C_col[m,n] = C_row[m,n] but stored at offset m + n*M. In a PyTorch row-major [M,N] tensor, element [m,n] is at m*N + n. But cuBLAS writes to m + n*M. So the memory layout is transposed! The values are correct but stored in a transposed layout. To fix this, we can either: 1. Transpose the result after cuBLAS 2. Or tell cuBLAS to compute C_col = B_col @ A_col, and then C_col corresponds to C_row^T? Let's see: If we compute C_col = B_col @ A_col where B_col is NxK and A_col is KxM: C_col = (NxK) @ (KxM) = NxM. C_col[n,m] = sum_k B_col[n,k] * A_col[k,m] = sum_k B_row[k,n] * A_row[m,k]. This is not the same as C_row[m,n] = sum_k A_row[m,k] * B_row[k,n] (multiplication commutes, so it IS the same numerically!). But C_col[n,m] = C_row[m,n], and C_col is stored in column-major NxM: C_col[n,m] at offset n + m*N. In PyTorch row-major [M,N], C_row[m,n] should be at m*N + n. Offset n + m*N = m*N + n. YES! They match! So if we compute C_col = B_col @ A_col with dimensions NxM, we get the correct layout! Wait, let me verify: cuBLAS computes C = op(B) @ op(A). If opB = N (no transpose), B is NxK col-major. op(B) = NxK. If opA = N (no transpose), A is KxM col-major. op(A) = KxM. Result C is NxM col-major. C[n,m] = sum_k B[n,k] * A[k,m]. Now, B[n,k] in col-major NxK = B_row[k,n] in row-major KxN. A[k,m] in col-major KxM = A_row[m,k] in row-major MxK. So C[n,m] = sum_k B_row[k,n] * A_row[m,k] = sum_k A_row[m,k] * B_row[k,n] = C_row[m,n]. And C is stored as NxM col-major: C[n,m] at offset n + m*N. In PyTorch row-major [M,N], C[m,n] is at offset m*N + n. Offset n + m*N = m*N + n. ✓ So the trick is: Call `cublas<t>gemmStridedBatched(handle, CUBLAS_OP_N, CUBLAS_OP_N, N, M, K, alpha, B, N, strideB, A, K, strideA, beta, C, N, strideC, batch_count)` Wait, the leading dimension of B: B is NxK col-major, so ldb = N. The leading dimension of A: A is KxM col-major, so lda = K. The leading dimension of C: C is NxM col-major, so ldc = N. This computes: C = B @ A, where C is NxM, B is NxK, A is KxM. In PyTorch terms: C[M,N] = A[M,K] @ B[K,N]. ✓ Great! So the correct cuBLAS call for row-standard row-major PyTorch tensors is: - Transpose A and B in the call (swap them): compute C = B @ A - No transpose flags - m = N, n = M, k = K - lda = N (B's leading dim), ldb = K (A's leading dim), ldc = N (C's leading dim) The batch strides: - strideA = K * M (A batch stride, since A is KxM col-major per batch) - strideB = N * K (B batch stride) - strideC = N * M (C batch stride) Wait, for per-batch pointers in strided batched: A_batch[b] = A + b * M * K (PyTorch row-major [B, M, K]) But cuBLAS views A as KxM col-major per batch. In col-major KxM, batch stride is K*M = M*K. So strideA = M*K. ✓ B_batch[b] = B + b * K * N (PyTorch row-major [B, K, N]) cuBLAS views B as NxK col-major. Batch stride is N*K = K*N. ✓ C_batch[b] = C + b * M * N cuBLAS views C as NxM col-major. Batch stride is N*M = M*N. ✓ Wait, but I just said A in cuBLAS is KxM col-major. For A originally [M, K] row-major: A_col_major[k,m] = A_row_major[m,k] = A_ptr[m*K + k]. But cuBLAS reads A_col_major[k,m] at offset k + m*K. If we pass A_ptr directly, cuBLAS reads offset k + m*K. But PyTorch A_ptr[m*K + k] = A[m,k]. When m=0, k=1: PyTorch has A[1], cuBLAS reads A[1]. When m=1, k=0: PyTorch has A[K], cuBLAS reads A[1]. They differ! So passing A_ptr directly doesn't work for row-major A. Wait, earlier I said A_row_major[M,K] = A_col_major^T[K,M]. If cuBLAS views the memory as KxM col-major, then A_col_major[k,m] = A_ptr[k + m*K]. But A_ptr was filled in row-major, so A_ptr[k + m*K] = A_row_major[m',k'] where m'*K + k' = k + m*K. For m'=k, k'=m*K: that's not integer for K>1. Actually, let me re-examine: if M=520, K=129. A_ptr row-major: A[m,k] at m*129 + k. If cuBLAS reads as KxM col-major: A_col[k,m] at k + m*129? No, col-major KxM has lda=K, so A_col[k,m] at k + m*K = k + m*129. Wait, k + m*129 for k∈[0,128], m∈[0,519]: When k=0, m=1: offset 129. In row-major, offset 129 = 1*129 + 0 = A[1,0]. So A_col[0,1] = A_row[1,0]. Which means A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k] if K=M, but in general A_col[k,m] corresponds to A_row[m,k] when the offset k + m*K equals m'*K + k'. Solving m'=m, k'=k gives k + m*K = m*K + k. YES! A_row[m, k] is at m*K + k. A_col[k, m] is at k + m*K. These are equal! So A_row_major[M,K] stored at offset m*K + k, when read as A_col_major[K,M] at offset k + m*K, gives A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k] for all m, k! Wait, is this true? For M=520, K=129: A_row[1, 0] = 1*129 + 0 = 129. A_col[0, 1] = 0 + 1*129 = 129. So A_col[0, 1] = A_row[1, 0]. But we wanted A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k]. So A_col[0,1] = A_row[1,0]. Wait, actually: offset = m*K + k = 1*129 + 0 = 129. And A_col[k,m] = A_col[0,1] at offset 0 + 1*129 = 129. So A_col[0,1] = memory[129] = A_row[1,0]. But for GEMM, we want A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k], because A_col represents the transpose of A_row. If A_col[0,1] = A_row[1,0], then this is indeed A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k] with k=0, m=1! Wait, A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k] means: A_col[0,1] should equal A_row[1,0]. And it does! (both at offset 129) A_col[1,0] should equal A_row[0,1]. A_col[1,0] is at offset 1 + 0*129 = 1. A_row[0,1] is at offset 0*129 + 1 = 1. Yes! So A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k] is satisfied! Because: A_col[k,m] at offset k + m*K = A_row[m,k] at offset m*K + k. Yes, they are equal! So viewing a row-major [M,K] tensor as a column-major [K,M] tensor correctly gives the transpose values! Wait, is this always true? Let me verify with general indices: A_row[m,k] at offset m*K + k. A_col[k,m] at offset k + m*K = m*K + k. Same offset. So A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k]. And since A_col is K×M, A_col[k,m] corresponds to row k, column m. In the transpose A_row^T, element (k,m) = A_row[m,k]. So A_col = A_row^T. YES! A row-major [M,K] tensor, when viewed as a column-major [K,M] matrix, is exactly the transpose of the logical values. This is because row-major [M,K] and column-major [K,M] have the same memory layout! Similarly: - Row-major [K,N] viewed as column-major [N,K] is the transpose. - Row-major [M,N] viewed as column-major [N,M] is the transpose. This is a well-known property: the transpose of a row-major matrix has the same memory layout as the original in column-major. So for cuBLAS: - Pass A_row [M,K] as a column-major [K,M] matrix. This gives logical values A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k] = A^T. - Pass B_row [K,N] as a column-major [N,K] matrix. This gives logical values B_col[n,k] = B_row[k,n] = B^T. Then compute C_col = B_col @ A_col = B^T @ A^T = (A @ B)^T. And C_col as column-major [N,M] is stored at offset n + m*N. In row-major [M,N], element [m,n] is at m*N + n. These are NOT the same! Offset n + m*N vs m*N + n. For m=1, n=1: same. For m=1, n=0: offset 1 vs 520. Different. So C_col = (A@B)^T is stored in memory as a column-major [N,M]. To get C_row = A@B in row-major [M,N], we need to transpose C_col. But earlier I showed that if we compute C_col = B_col @ A_col, we get C_col[n,m] = sum_k B_col[n,k] * A_col[k,m] = sum_k B_row[k,n] * A_row[m,k] = C_row[m,n]. And C_col is stored as col-major NxM: offset n + m*N. Row-major [M,N] would need C_row[m,n] at m*N + n. Wait, m*N + n vs n + m*N. They ARE the same! m*N + n = n + m*N. Addition is commutative! I was confused earlier. n + m*N = m*N + n. Yes, they are the same offset! So C_col[n,m] at offset n + m*N = m*N + n. And in row-major [M,N], C_row[m,n] is at m*N + n. The offsets match! So C_col[n,m] is stored at the same memory location as C_row[m,n]. And we computed C_col[n,m] = C_row[m,n] = sum_k A_row[m,k] * B_row[k,n]. So if we compute C_col = B_col @ A_col where: - B_col is NxK col-major (view of B_row [K,N]) - A_col is KxM col-major (view of A_row [M,K]) - C_col is NxM col-major (view of C_row [M,N]) Then the result is exactly C = A @ B in standard row-major! Wait, but cuBLAS computes C = op(B) * op(A), where the first operand is B and second is A. So: cublasGemmStridedBatched(handle, opB, opA, N, M, K, alpha, B_ptr, ldb, strideB, A_ptr, lda, strideA, beta, C_ptr, ldc, strideC, batch) If opB = N (no transpose), opA = N (no transpose): - First operand: B_ptr, treated as N×K col-major (ldb = N) - Second operand: A_ptr, treated as K×M col-major (lda = K) - Result: C_ptr, N×M col-major (ldc = N) This computes C = B @ A where B is N×K and A is K×M. Result C is N×M. And as we showed, C_col = B_col @ A_col gives the correct row-major result. Wait, but I said B_col[n,k] = B_row[k,n]. And A_col[k,m] = A_row[m,k]. Then C_col[n,m] = sum_k B_col[n,k] * A_col[k,m] = sum_k B_row[k,n] * A_row[m,k] = sum_k A_row[m,k] * B_row[k,n] = C_row[m,n]. YES! And C_col[n,m] stored at offset n + m*N = m*N + n = C_row[m,n] offset. So the cuBLAS call parameters for row-standard row-major PyTorch tensors are: - Transpose parameters: CUBLAS_OP_N, CUBLAS_OP_N - m = N (rows of C) - n = M (cols of C) - k = K (inner dimension) - A (second operand): K×M col-major, lda = K - B (first operand): N×K col-major, ldb = N - C: N×M col-major, ldc = N The batch strides: - A: M * K - B: K * N - C: M * N Let me double-check with a small example: M=2, K=2, N=2. A_row = [[a00, a01], [a10, a11]] (row-major) B_row = [[b00, b01], [b10, b11]] (row-major) C_row = A @ B = [[a00*b00 + a01*b10, a00*b01 + a01*b11], [a10*b00 + a11*b10, a10*b01 + a11*b11]] A as K×M col-major = [[a00, a10], [a01, a11]] B as N×K col-major = [[b00, b10], [b01, b11]] B @ A = [[b00, b10], [b01, b11]] @ [[a00, a10], [a01, a11]] = [[b00*a00 + b10*a01, b00*a10 + b10*a11], [b01*a00 + b11*a01, b01*a10 + b11*a11]] = [[a00*b00 + a01*b10, a10*b00 + a11*b10], [a00*b01 + a01*b11, a10*b01 + a11*b11]] As N×M col-major (N=2, M=2): C_col = [[c00, c10], [c01, c11]] stored as [c00, c10, c01, c11]. But we want C_row = [[c00, c01], [c10, c11]] stored as [c00, c01, c10, c11]. Wait, C_col[n,m] for n=0,m=0: c00 = a00*b00 + a01*b10 ✓ C_col[n,m] for n=1,m=0: c01 = a00*b01 + a01*b11 ✓ C_col[n,m] for n=0,m=1: c10 = a10*b00 + a11*b10 ✓ C_col[n,m] for n=1,m=1: c11 = a10*b01 + a11*b11 ✓ C_col stored as col-major N×M: offset(0,0)=0, offset(0,1)=1? No. Col-major N×M: element (n,m) at offset n + m*N. (0,0): 0 (1,0): 1 (0,1): 0 + 1*2 = 2 (1,1): 1 + 1*2 = 3 So memory: [C_col[0,0], C_col[1,0], C_col[0,1], C_col[1,1]] = [c00, c01, c10, c11]. And C_row in row-major [M,N]: element (m,n) at m*N + n. (0,0): 0 (0,1): 1 (1,0): 2 (1,1): 3 Memory: [C_row[0,0], C_row[0,1], C_row[1,0], C_row[1,1]] = [c00, c01, c10, c11]. The memory layouts MATCH! So cuBLAS call: - handle - CUBLAS_OP_N, CUBLAS_OP_N (opB, opA) - N, M, K (m, n, k in cuBLAS terms for the result) - alpha - B_ptr, N, N*K (first operand, ldb, stride) - A_ptr, K, M*K (second operand, lda, stride) - beta - C_ptr, N, M*N (result, ldc, stride) - batch_count This computes: C = B @ A, where B is N×K and A is K×M, giving C as N×M. And as we showed, the memory layout of C is exactly C_row [M,N] row-major! Now I need to incorporate C0 (the initial C for beta*C0). In cuBLAS, the formula is: C = alpha * (B @ A) + beta * C So I can pass C0 as the C matrix, and cuBLAS will accumulate into it. But cuBLAS modifies the C matrix in-place. If C0 is a separate tensor and I don't want to modify it, I should copy C0 to C first. Or I can create C as a copy of C0 and pass it to cuBLAS. For FP16: I can do the GEMM in FP16 using `cublasGemmStridedBatchedEx`. Actually, for cuBLAS to do the accumulation in FP32 while computing in FP16, I should use `CUBLAS_COMPUTE_32F` or similar. Actually, on H100, for optimal FP16 tensor core performance, I should use `CUBLAS_COMPUTE_16F` or `CUBLAS_COMPUTE_32F_FAST_16F`. Let me use `CUBLAS_COMPUTE_32F` for better accuracy. In the formula C = op(A) * op(B): - A is first matrix operand - B is second matrix operand But in my earlier analysis, I said cuBLAS computes C = op(B) * op(A) where I pass B as first and A as second. Let me re-read. cuBLAS `cublas<t>gemm` computes: C = alpha * op(A) * op(B) + beta * C Where: - op(A) is m×k - op(B) is k×n - C is m×n So the first operand corresponds to m (rows of C), and the second to n (cols of C). For row-standard C = A @ B where C is M×N: - We need C to be M×N in row-major - As shown, this is equivalent to C_col (N×M col-major) = B_col (N×K col-major) @ A_col (K×M col-major) So in cuBLAS: - op(A) = B_col, size N×K - op(B) = A_col, size K×M - C = N×M Thus: - transa = CUBLAS_OP_N - transb = CUBLAS_OP_N - m = N (rows of C, which is rows of op(A)) - n = M (cols of C, which is cols of op(B)) - k = K (inner dim) The first operand (A in cuBLAS terms) is B_col (NxK). lda = N (leading dim of B_col). strideA = K * N. The second operand (B in cuBLAS terms) is A_col (KxM). ldb = K (leading dim of A_col). strideB = M * K. C is NxM col-major. ldc = N. strideC = M * N. So the call is: ```cpp cublasGemmStridedBatchedEx(handle, CUBLAS_OP_N, CUBLAS_OP_N, // transa, transb N, M, K, // m, n, k &alpha, B_half, CUDA_R_16F, N, K * N, // A operand A_half, CUDA_R_16F, K, M * K, // B operand &beta, C_float, CUDA_R_32F, N, M * N, // C output batch, CUBLAS_COMPUTE_32F, CUBLAS_GEMM_DEFAULT_TENSOR_OP); ``` This computes C = alpha * B_half @ A_half + beta * C_float. import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline

CPP_SOURCE = r'''
torch::Tensor batched_gemm_forward(torch::Tensor A, torch::Tensor B, torch::Tensor C0, float alpha, float beta);
'''

CUDA_SOURCE = r'''
#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <cuda_fp16.h>

__global__ void batched_gemm_fp16_kernel(
    const half* __restrict__ A,
    const half* __restrict__ B,
    const half* __restrict__ C0,
    half* __restrict__ C,
    int M, int N, int K,
    int batch_count,
    float alpha, float beta) {

  int batch = blockIdx.z;
  if (batch >= batch_count) return;

  int m = blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y;
  int n = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;

  if (m >= M || n >= N) return;

  const half* A_batch = A + (int64_t)batch * M * K;
  const half* B_batch = B + (int64_t)batch * K * N;
  const half* C0_batch = C0 + (int64_t)batch * M * N;
  half* C_batch = C + (int64_t)batch * M * N;

  float acc = 0.0f;
  for (int k = 0; k < K; ++k) {
    float a = __half2float(A_batch[m * K + k]);
    float b = __half2float(B_batch[k * N + n]);
    acc += a * b;
  }

  float c0 = __half2float(C0_batch[m * N + n]);
  C_batch[m * N + n] = __float2half(alpha * acc + beta * c0);
}

torch::Tensor batched_gemm_forward(
    torch::Tensor A,
    torch::Tensor B,
    torch::Tensor C0,
    float alpha,
    float beta) {
  int batch = A.size(0);
  int M = A.size(1);
  int K = A.size(2);
  int N = B.size(2);

  auto C = torch::empty({batch, M, N}, A.options());

  dim3 threads(16, 16);
  dim3 blocks((N + 15) / 16, (M + 15) / 16, batch);

  batched_gemm_fp16_kernel<<<blocks, threads>>>(
      reinterpret_cast<const half*>(A.data_ptr<at::Half>()),
      reinterpret_cast<const half*>(B.data_ptr<at::Half>()),
      reinterpret_cast<const half*>(C0.data_ptr<at::Half>()),
      reinterpret_cast<half*>(C.data_ptr<at::Half>()),
      M, N, K, batch, alpha, beta);

  return C;
}
'''

batched_gemm = load_inline(
    name="batched_gemm_fp16",
    cpp_sources=CPP_SOURCE,
    cuda_sources=CUDA_SOURCE,
    functions=["batched_gemm_forward"],
    extra_cuda_cflags=["-O3", "-gencode=arch=compute_90,code=sm_90"],
    verbose=False,
).batched_gemm_forward


class ModelNew(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, BATCH, K, N, ALPHA, BETA):
        super().__init__()
        self.N = N
        self.alpha = ALPHA
        self.beta = BETA
        self.B = nn.Parameter(torch.randn(BATCH, K, N, dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda'))

    def forward(self, A):
        batch, M, K = A.shape
        A_half = A.half()
        C0 = torch.ones(batch, M, self.N, dtype=torch.float16, device=A.device)
        return batched_gemm(A_half, self.B, C0, self.alpha, self.beta).float()
